Regression - Chapter 219

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a regression algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 717. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 863/5.

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_717(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 717. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^863(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 219.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the regression structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 631. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 155 and converges at rate O(1/n^3).

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the structural decomposition framework applied to regression structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 631/155 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 219.8 (Compactness Result)

Statement: Under the compactness result assumption on regression structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 881. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 633 with relations in degree 5.

For the inductive step, we decompose the regression structure using the compactness result theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 633. Each subproblem has a solution by the compactness result fixed-point theorem with constant 5. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
